{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Mxn157tctdEsAtEMwpH99k","type":"tool_result","content":"=== docs/boost_programme_brief.md\n# Boost — programme brief\n\n**What it is.** Boost attaches a guaranteed minimum payout to an individual delivery offer. A courier who\naccepts a Boost offer is paid at least the guaranteed floor for that delivery regardless of distance or\nwaiting time. Unboosted offers are paid on the standard distance-and-time formula.\n\n**Why Operations proposed it.** Couriers deliberate over marginal offers, particularly in the evening peak.\nDeliberation shows up as acceptance latency, and acceptance latency is the part of the delivery clock the\nplatform controls. The programme hypothesis is that removing payout uncertainty from the offer shortens\nacceptance latency and therefore reduces late deliveries.\n\n**What it costs.** The guarantee only binds on offers whose standard payout would have fallen below the\nfloor. Realised incentive spend per boosted order is recorded in `incentive_ledger`.\n\n**What it does not change.** Boost does not change the promise window shown to the customer, the courier\npay formula for unboosted offers, restaurant preparation, or the dispatch radius.\n=== docs/courier_supply_note.md\n# Courier supply — scheduling note\n\nCouriers choose their own hours. They are not rostered. The scheduling team reviews online hours weekly and\nhas consistently found that online hours in a market track **expected earnings per online hour** in that\nmarket over the preceding days: couriers come online where the week has been paying, and stop early where it\nhas not.\n\nCouriers see the guarantee as a property of the market they are working, not of an individual offer — the\nin-app earnings summary reports realised earnings per hour for the week to date, by market.\n\nOnline hours per courier per clock hour are in `courier_shifts`. Nothing in the platform caps the number of\ncouriers who may be online in a market-hour.\n\nA denser pool of available couriers shortens the distance from the accepting courier to the pickup point.\nThe dispatch radius is unchanged by Boost.\n=== docs/data_dictionary.md\n# Warehouse — data dictionary\n\n`data/northline.sqlite`, read-only.\n\n## markets\n`market_id` · `market_name` · `region` · `launched_on` · `zone_count`\n\n## zones\n`zone_id` · `market_id` — the market this zone belongs to · `zone_name`\n\n## orders\n`order_id` · `zone_id` · `placed_at` — local platform time ·\n`order_channel` — `standard`, `scheduled` or `corporate`; only `standard` orders are Boost-eligible ·\n`promised_minutes` — the promise shown to the customer · `accepted_after_sec` — seconds from queue entry to courier acceptance; null for cancelled orders ·\n`delivered_after_min` — minutes from `placed_at` to delivery; null for cancelled orders ·\n`status` — `delivered` or `cancelled`\n\n## experiment_assignment\n`order_id` · `experiment_id` · `arm` — `boost`, `control` or `excluded`. Rows exist only for orders placed in\nan enrolled market during phase 2.\n\n## courier_shifts\n`shift_id` · `courier_id` · `market_id` · `shift_date` · `hour_start` — clock hour · `online_minutes`\n\n## experiment_config\n`experiment_id` · `market_id` · `week_start` · `phase` — `pre`, `phase1_soak`, `phase2_order_randomised` ·\n`randomisation_unit` — `none`, `market`, `order` · `boost_share_target` — configured share of eligible orders\ncarrying Boost · `status` — `not_running`, `running`, `not_enrolled`, `holdout`\n\n## market_week_baseline\n`market_id` · `week_start` · `orders` · `late_orders` · `median_delivered_min`. Pre-programme weeks only.\n\n## incentive_ledger\n`market_id` · `week_start` · `boosted_orders` · `incentive_spend_gbp`\n\n## ops_events\n`event_id` · `event_date` · `market_id` — null when platform-wide · `event_type` · `note`\n=== docs/dispatch_offer_queue.md\n# Dispatcher — offer queue\n\n## How an order reaches a courier\n\nWhen an order is ready to dispatch it enters the **offer queue** for its market and hour. The dispatcher\nholds one queue per market-hour. Couriers who are online and not currently carrying a delivery are offered\nthe head of the queue.\n\nOrdering within the queue: **a Boost offer is placed ahead of every unboosted offer in the same market-hour\nqueue.** Within the boosted group, and within the unboosted group, offers are ordered by readiness time.\n\nA courier carries one delivery at a time. A courier who accepts an offer is unavailable until that delivery\ncompletes, at which point they return to the front of the available pool.\n\n`orders.accepted_after_sec` is the elapsed time from the order entering the queue to a courier accepting it.\n\n## Dispatcher 4.11\n\nThe 4.11 release added offer-queue telemetry. It did not change queue ordering, the offer radius, or the\npay formula. See `ops_events`.\n=== docs/experiment_plan.md\n# Boost experiment plan (EXP-BOOST-01)\n\n## Phase 1 — soak\n\nBoost was enabled for **every eligible order** in the phase-1 \"on\" markets for four weeks. Sixteen markets\nwere enrolled in the programme; the phase-1 on/off split was drawn by coin flip over the enrolled markets\nbefore the phase opened, eight on and eight off. Four further markets were never enrolled in the programme\nat any phase.\n\nPhase 1 was scheduled to confirm that the guarantee did not create payout or fraud incidents. It closed on\nschedule with no incidents raised (`ops_events`).\n\n## Phase 2 — order-randomised\n\nFrom the phase-2 start date the dispatcher attached Boost to an individual eligible order with the\nconfigured probability, drawn independently per order. `experiment_config.boost_share_target` records the\nconfigured probability for each market-week; `experiment_assignment.arm` records what each order actually\ngot. The probability was raised once, after the earnings-volatility review, on the date in `ops_events`.\n\nThe four never-enrolled markets remained off Boost throughout phase 2 and carry no assignment rows.\n\n## Eligibility and exclusions\n\nScheduled-ahead and corporate-account orders are not eligible for Boost. They carry\n`order_channel` other than `standard`, and in phase 2 they are also recorded with `arm = 'excluded'`.\n\n## Balance\n\n`market_week_baseline` holds market-week orders, late orders and median delivery time for the four weeks\nbefore phase 1. Markets differ persistently in their delivery performance for reasons that predate the\nprogramme — road network, restaurant density, courier mix — so these weeks are the reference for what each\nmarket looked like before anything changed.\n=== docs/metric_definitions.md\n# Metric definitions\n\n## Late-delivery rate\n\nAn order is **late** when `delivered_after_min` exceeds `promised_minutes`. The late-delivery rate over any\nset of orders is late orders divided by orders in that set.\n\n**Population.** Only orders with `status = 'delivered'` enter the metric. Cancelled orders have no delivery\ntime and are excluded; they are not counted as late.\n\n**Eligibility.** Programme reporting covers **Boost-eligible** orders only: `order_channel = 'standard'`.\nScheduled-ahead and corporate orders are never eligible for Boost, in any period, and are outside the\nprogramme metric. `order_channel` is recorded on every order, including the weeks before the programme, so\nthe same population can be built for any period. This is the definition the service-level report and the\nincentive ledger both use.\n\n## Grain and calendar\n\nThe reporting grain for programme metrics is the **market-week**. Weeks start on Monday, 00:00, and are\nlabelled by that Monday's date — the same `week_start` convention used in `experiment_config`,\n`market_week_baseline` and `incentive_ledger`. Order timestamps are local platform time with no offset\nchanges in the programme window.\n\n## Market attribution\n\nOrders carry `zone_id`, not `market_id`. A zone belongs to exactly one market for the whole programme\nwindow; join `orders` to `zones` to attribute an order to a market.\n\n## Courier hours\n\n`courier_shifts` holds one row per courier per clock hour with the minutes that courier was online in that\nhour. Courier hours for a market-hour are the sum of `online_minutes` over that market, date and hour,\ndivided by 60.\n=== docs/rollout_decision_memo.md\n# Boost — rollout decision memo (Finance & Service Operations)\n\n## The decision\n\nThe July investment committee will either approve Boost for **national rollout on every eligible order in\nevery market**, or decline it. There is no partial-rollout option on the table: the guarantee is a published\ncourier pay term and Operations will not run it in some markets and not others beyond the pilot.\n\n## The quantity the decision is made on\n\nThe committee is deciding whether to turn Boost on for every eligible order in every enrolled market.\nThe number it needs is therefore:\n\n> **The change in our estate-wide late-delivery rate — total late deliveries divided by total eligible\n> deliveries across the enrolled markets — between running Boost on every eligible order and running it on\n> none, under trading conditions like those of the programme period.**\n\nExpress it in percentage points, negative for an improvement.\n\nTwo points of definition, so that everyone is quoting the same number:\n\n- **Estate-wide, not an average of markets.** A market doing ten times the volume matters ten times as much\n  to the P&L and to the service-credit bill. Where a market-by-market figure has to be combined, weight each\n  market by **its share of estate orders in the four weeks before the programme opened**. We fix the weights\n  on the pre-programme mix on purpose: Boost could itself shift where orders land, and the weighting must not\n  move with the thing being measured.\n- **Trading conditions like the programme period.** This is the effect under conditions comparable to those\n  we actually observed. Carrying it forward to a different season is a judgement for the committee, not part\n  of the number.\n\n## The threshold\n\nBoost is only worth buying if the late deliveries it avoids are worth more than the incentive it pays.\n\n- **Incentive cost.** Realised incentive spend per boosted order, from `incentive_ledger`, is £0.19.\n- **Cost of a late delivery.** The service-credit schedule pays the customer £2.50. Retention analysis\n  values the expected future margin lost on a late delivery at £10.20. Total **£12.70**.\n\nBoost therefore pays for itself when it avoids more than `0.19 / 12.70` of a late delivery per order, i.e.\nwhen the late-delivery rate falls by at least **1.4961 percentage points**.\n\n**Decision rule.**\n\n- `roll_out` — the estimated change is a reduction of at least the break-even figure above, **and** the 95 %\n  interval on that estimate excludes zero.\n- `do_not_roll_out` — otherwise.\n=== docs/outputs/readout_contract.md\n# Output contract\n\nWrite `out/readout.json` and `out/market_week_panel.csv`.\n\n## `out/market_week_panel.csv`\n\nOne row per market-week over the whole warehouse window. Columns, in this order:\n\n`market_id`, `week_start`, `orders`, `late_orders`, `late_rate_pct`, `boost_share`, `courier_hours`\n\n`orders` and `late_orders` follow the programme metric population in `docs/metric_definitions.md`.\n`boost_share` is the share of that market-week's metric-population orders that carried Boost.\n`courier_hours` is defined in `docs/metric_definitions.md`.\n\n## `out/readout.json`\n\nA single JSON object with exactly these keys:\n\n| key | type | |\n|---|---|---|\n| `orders_analysed` | int | orders in the programme metric population, phase 2, enrolled markets |\n| `order_arm_contrast_pp` | number | the difference in late-delivery rate between the phase-2 arms, in percentage points |\n| `programme_effect_pp` | number | the quantity named in `docs/rollout_decision_memo.md` |\n| `programme_effect_ci_low_pp` | number | 95 % interval on that quantity, lower bound |\n| `programme_effect_ci_high_pp` | number | 95 % interval, upper bound |\n| `inference_unit_n` | int | how many independent units your interval is computed over |\n| `courier_hours_response_pct` | number | the percentage change in courier hours per order attributable to Boost being on rather than off |\n| `decision` | string | `roll_out` or `do_not_roll_out` |\n\nPercentage-point fields are signed: negative means the late-delivery rate falls.\n=== reports/boost_readout_2026-06.md\n# Boost — programme readout\n\n**Prepared by** Marketplace Analytics · **For** Service Operations and Finance\n\n## Headline\n\nBoost reduces the late-delivery rate by **4.0 percentage points** (95 % CI 3.6 to 4.4 pp), measured over\n120,671 eligible delivered orders in the sixteen enrolled markets across the six phase-2 weeks.\n\nAgainst the break-even figure of 1.50 pp in the rollout memo, Boost clears the bar by a wide margin.\n\n**Recommendation: roll out.**\n\n## Method\n\nPhase 2 randomised Boost per order, so the boost and control arms are directly comparable. We take the\nlate-delivery rate in each arm over the metric population and difference them, with a two-proportion 95 %\ninterval. Reproduce with `python -m northline_eval readout`.\n\n## Checks\n\n| check | result |\n|---|---|\n| Sample ratio, realised vs configured share, 96 market-weeks | max abs z = 2.09 — no mismatch |\n| Pre-programme balance, enrolled vs never-enrolled markets | 14.55 % vs 15.30 % late — comparable |\n| Per-market consistency, all 16 markets | every market negative, range −3.4 to −4.6 pp |\n| Courier hours available per arm | boost 9.53 h, control 9.54 h, gap −0.08 % |\n\nThe courier-hours check is the one we were asked about most. Courier supply is the obvious thing that could\ndiffer between the arms, and it does not: the two arms draw on the same courier hours to within a tenth of a\npercent. **Capacity is therefore not confounding the comparison.**\n\n## Control arm against the never-enrolled markets\n\nThe memo requires this alongside the headline. The phase-2 control arm ran at 17.26 % late against 14.82 % in\nthe four never-enrolled markets, a difference of +2.44 pp.\n\nWe do not read this as evidence about Boost. The never-enrolled markets were never part of the programme;\nthey were not drawn against the enrolled markets and they are not a control group. A difference in level\nbetween two groups that were never randomised against each other is not an effect, and we would not report it\nas one.\n\n## Limitations\n\nSix weeks of phase 2. We have not modelled courier-side effects beyond the capacity check above.\n=== notebooks/analyst_note.md\n# Working note — Boost readout\n\n**Why the phase-2 design is the one to use.** Phase 1 was a three-week soak in eight markets, scheduled to\ncatch payout and fraud incidents before we exposed the whole estate. It is only three weeks, it is sixteen\nmarkets at best, and a market-level comparison across eight and eight gives an interval several points wide —\nuseless for a committee decision. Phase 2 randomised per order over six weeks and 120k orders, which is why\nthe interval is four tenths of a point rather than several points. I used phase 2.\n\n**Things I checked and then stopped worrying about.**\n\n- Assignment looks clean. Realised boost share tracks the configured target in every market-week; the largest\n  z across 96 market-weeks is 2.09, which is what you expect from 96 draws.\n- The enrolled and never-enrolled markets were comparable before the programme opened (14.55 vs 15.30 % late).\n- The effect is present in all sixteen markets, between −3.4 and −4.6 pp. A single market or a single week is\n  not driving it.\n- Courier supply per arm is identical to within 0.08 %. This was the one I expected to find something in, and\n  there is nothing there, so whatever Boost is doing it is not doing it by changing how many couriers we have.\n\n**Open.** The control arm is 2.4 pp worse than the never-enrolled markets. Those markets were never\nrandomised against the enrolled ones, so I do not think a level gap between them tells us anything, and I have\nleft it at that. Worth a second look if anyone has time before the committee, but it does not change the\nheadline.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"678e3b94-195a-474e-8237-0b1ae1c201c6","uuid":"66223f7e-b2cb-45dd-a0df-5e045dd29dd4","timestamp":"2026-10-01T08:10:01.827Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"=== docs/boost_programme_brief.md\n# Boost — programme brief\n\n**What it is.** Boost attaches a guaranteed minimum payout to an individual delivery offer. A courier who\naccepts a Boost offer is paid at least the guaranteed floor for that delivery regardless of distance or\nwaiting time. Unboosted offers are paid on the standard distance-and-time formula.\n\n**Why Operations proposed it.** Couriers deliberate over marginal offers, particularly in the evening peak.\nDeliberation shows up as acceptance latency, and acceptance latency is the part of the delivery clock the\nplatform controls. The programme hypothesis is that removing payout uncertainty from the offer shortens\nacceptance latency and therefore reduces late deliveries.\n\n**What it costs.** The guarantee only binds on offers whose standard payout would have fallen below the\nfloor. Realised incentive spend per boosted order is recorded in `incentive_ledger`.\n\n**What it does not change.** Boost does not change the promise window shown to the customer, the courier\npay formula for unboosted offers, restaurant preparation, or the dispatch radius.\n=== docs/courier_supply_note.md\n# Courier supply — scheduling note\n\nCouriers choose their own hours. They are not rostered. The scheduling team reviews online hours weekly and\nhas consistently found that online hours in a market track **expected earnings per online hour** in that\nmarket over the preceding days: couriers come online where the week has been paying, and stop early where it\nhas not.\n\nCouriers see the guarantee as a property of the market they are working, not of an individual offer — the\nin-app earnings summary reports realised earnings per hour for the week to date, by market.\n\nOnline hours per courier per clock hour are in `courier_shifts`. Nothing in the platform caps the number of\ncouriers who may be online in a market-hour.\n\nA denser pool of available couriers shortens the distance from the accepting courier to the pickup point.\nThe dispatch radius is unchanged by Boost.\n=== docs/data_dictionary.md\n# Warehouse — data dictionary\n\n`data/northline.sqlite`, read-only.\n\n## markets\n`market_id` · `market_name` · `region` · `launched_on` · `zone_count`\n\n## zones\n`zone_id` · `market_id` — the market this zone belongs to · `zone_name`\n\n## orders\n`order_id` · `zone_id` · `placed_at` — local platform time ·\n`order_channel` — `standard`, `scheduled` or `corporate`; only `standard` orders are Boost-eligible ·\n`promised_minutes` — the promise shown to the customer · `accepted_after_sec` — seconds from queue entry to courier acceptance; null for cancelled orders ·\n`delivered_after_min` — minutes from `placed_at` to delivery; null for cancelled orders ·\n`status` — `delivered` or `cancelled`\n\n## experiment_assignment\n`order_id` · `experiment_id` · `arm` — `boost`, `control` or `excluded`. Rows exist only for orders placed in\nan enrolled market during phase 2.\n\n## courier_shifts\n`shift_id` · `courier_id` · `market_id` · `shift_date` · `hour_start` — clock hour · `online_minutes`\n\n## experiment_config\n`experiment_id` · `market_id` · `week_start` · `phase` — `pre`, `phase1_soak`, `phase2_order_randomised` ·\n`randomisation_unit` — `none`, `market`, `order` · `boost_share_target` — configured share of eligible orders\ncarrying Boost · `status` — `not_running`, `running`, `not_enrolled`, `holdout`\n\n## market_week_baseline\n`market_id` · `week_start` · `orders` · `late_orders` · `median_delivered_min`. Pre-programme weeks only.\n\n## incentive_ledger\n`market_id` · `week_start` · `boosted_orders` · `incentive_spend_gbp`\n\n## ops_events\n`event_id` · `event_date` · `market_id` — null when platform-wide · `event_type` · `note`\n=== docs/dispatch_offer_queue.md\n# Dispatcher — offer queue\n\n## How an order reaches a courier\n\nWhen an order is ready to dispatch it enters the **offer queue** for its market and hour. The dispatcher\nholds one queue per market-hour. Couriers who are online and not currently carrying a delivery are offered\nthe head of the queue.\n\nOrdering within the queue: **a Boost offer is placed ahead of every unboosted offer in the same market-hour\nqueue.** Within the boosted group, and within the unboosted group, offers are ordered by readiness time.\n\nA courier carries one delivery at a time. A courier who accepts an offer is unavailable until that delivery\ncompletes, at which point they return to the front of the available pool.\n\n`orders.accepted_after_sec` is the elapsed time from the order entering the queue to a courier accepting it.\n\n## Dispatcher 4.11\n\nThe 4.11 release added offer-queue telemetry. It did not change queue ordering, the offer radius, or the\npay formula. See `ops_events`.\n=== docs/experiment_plan.md\n# Boost experiment plan (EXP-BOOST-01)\n\n## Phase 1 — soak\n\nBoost was enabled for **every eligible order** in the phase-1 \"on\" markets for four weeks. Sixteen markets\nwere enrolled in the programme; the phase-1 on/off split was drawn by coin flip over the enrolled markets\nbefore the phase opened, eight on and eight off. Four further markets were never enrolled in the programme\nat any phase.\n\nPhase 1 was scheduled to confirm that the guarantee did not create payout or fraud incidents. It closed on\nschedule with no incidents raised (`ops_events`).\n\n## Phase 2 — order-randomised\n\nFrom the phase-2 start date the dispatcher attached Boost to an individual eligible order with the\nconfigured probability, drawn independently per order. `experiment_config.boost_share_target` records the\nconfigured probability for each market-week; `experiment_assignment.arm` records what each order actually\ngot. The probability was raised once, after the earnings-volatility review, on the date in `ops_events`.\n\nThe four never-enrolled markets remained off Boost throughout phase 2 and carry no assignment rows.\n\n## Eligibility and exclusions\n\nScheduled-ahead and corporate-account orders are not eligible for Boost. They carry\n`order_channel` other than `standard`, and in phase 2 they are also recorded with `arm = 'excluded'`.\n\n## Balance\n\n`market_week_baseline` holds market-week orders, late orders and median delivery time for the four weeks\nbefore phase 1. Markets differ persistently in their delivery performance for reasons that predate the\nprogramme — road network, restaurant density, courier mix — so these weeks are the reference for what each\nmarket looked like before anything changed.\n=== docs/metric_definitions.md\n# Metric definitions\n\n## Late-delivery rate\n\nAn order is **late** when `delivered_after_min` exceeds `promised_minutes`. The late-delivery rate over any\nset of orders is late orders divided by orders in that set.\n\n**Population.** Only orders with `status = 'delivered'` enter the metric. Cancelled orders have no delivery\ntime and are excluded; they are not counted as late.\n\n**Eligibility.** Programme reporting covers **Boost-eligible** orders only: `order_channel = 'standard'`.\nScheduled-ahead and corporate orders are never eligible for Boost, in any period, and are outside the\nprogramme metric. `order_channel` is recorded on every order, including the weeks before the programme, so\nthe same population can be built for any period. This is the definition the service-level report and the\nincentive ledger both use.\n\n## Grain and calendar\n\nThe reporting grain for programme metrics is the **market-week**. Weeks start on Monday, 00:00, and are\nlabelled by that Monday's date — the same `week_start` convention used in `experiment_config`,\n`market_week_baseline` and `incentive_ledger`. Order timestamps are local platform time with no offset\nchanges in the programme window.\n\n## Market attribution\n\nOrders carry `zone_id`, not `market_id`. A zone belongs to exactly one market for the whole programme\nwindow; join `orders` to `zones` to attribute an order to a market.\n\n## Courier hours\n\n`courier_shifts` holds one row per courier per clock hour with the minutes that courier was online in that\nhour. Courier hours for a market-hour are the sum of `online_minutes` over that market, date and hour,\ndivided by 60.\n=== docs/rollout_decision_memo.md\n# Boost — rollout decision memo (Finance & Service Operations)\n\n## The decision\n\nThe July investment committee will either approve Boost for **national rollout on every eligible order in\nevery market**, or decline it. There is no partial-rollout option on the table: the guarantee is a published\ncourier pay term and Operations will not run it in some markets and not others beyond the pilot.\n\n## The quantity the decision is made on\n\nThe committee is deciding whether to turn Boost on for every eligible order in every enrolled market.\nThe number it needs is therefore:\n\n> **The change in our estate-wide late-delivery rate — total late deliveries divided by total eligible\n> deliveries across the enrolled markets — between running Boost on every eligible order and running it on\n> none, under trading conditions like those of the programme period.**\n\nExpress it in percentage points, negative for an improvement.\n\nTwo points of definition, so that everyone is quoting the same number:\n\n- **Estate-wide, not an average of markets.** A market doing ten times the volume matters ten times as much\n  to the P&L and to the service-credit bill. Where a market-by-market figure has to be combined, weight each\n  market by **its share of estate orders in the four weeks before the programme opened**. We fix the weights\n  on the pre-programme mix on purpose: Boost could itself shift where orders land, and the weighting must not\n  move with the thing being measured.\n- **Trading conditions like the programme period.** This is the effect under conditions comparable to those\n  we actually observed. Carrying it forward to a different season is a judgement for the committee, not part\n  of the number.\n\n## The threshold\n\nBoost is only worth buying if the late deliveries it avoids are worth more than the incentive it pays.\n\n- **Incentive cost.** Realised incentive spend per boosted order, from `incentive_ledger`, is £0.19.\n- **Cost of a late delivery.** The service-credit schedule pays the customer £2.50. Retention analysis\n  values the expected future margin lost on a late delivery at £10.20. Total **£12.70**.\n\nBoost therefore pays for itself when it avoids more than `0.19 / 12.70` of a late delivery per order, i.e.\nwhen the late-delivery rate falls by at least **1.4961 percentage points**.\n\n**Decision rule.**\n\n- `roll_out` — the estimated change is a reduction of at least the break-even figure above, **and** the 95 %\n  interval on that estimate excludes zero.\n- `do_not_roll_out` — otherwise.\n=== docs/outputs/readout_contract.md\n# Output contract\n\nWrite `out/readout.json` and `out/market_week_panel.csv`.\n\n## `out/market_week_panel.csv`\n\nOne row per market-week over the whole warehouse window. Columns, in this order:\n\n`market_id`, `week_start`, `orders`, `late_orders`, `late_rate_pct`, `boost_share`, `courier_hours`\n\n`orders` and `late_orders` follow the programme metric population in `docs/metric_definitions.md`.\n`boost_share` is the share of that market-week's metric-population orders that carried Boost.\n`courier_hours` is defined in `docs/metric_definitions.md`.\n\n## `out/readout.json`\n\nA single JSON object with exactly these keys:\n\n| key | type | |\n|---|---|---|\n| `orders_analysed` | int | orders in the programme metric population, phase 2, enrolled markets |\n| `order_arm_contrast_pp` | number | the difference in late-delivery rate between the phase-2 arms, in percentage points |\n| `programme_effect_pp` | number | the quantity named in `docs/rollout_decision_memo.md` |\n| `programme_effect_ci_low_pp` | number | 95 % interval on that quantity, lower bound |\n| `programme_effect_ci_high_pp` | number | 95 % interval, upper bound |\n| `inference_unit_n` | int | how many independent units your interval is computed over |\n| `courier_hours_response_pct` | number | the percentage change in courier hours per order attributable to Boost being on rather than off |\n| `decision` | string | `roll_out` or `do_not_roll_out` |\n\nPercentage-point fields are signed: negative means the late-delivery rate falls.\n=== reports/boost_readout_2026-06.md\n# Boost — programme readout\n\n**Prepared by** Marketplace Analytics · **For** Service Operations and Finance\n\n## Headline\n\nBoost reduces the late-delivery rate by **4.0 percentage points** (95 % CI 3.6 to 4.4 pp), measured over\n120,671 eligible delivered orders in the sixteen enrolled markets across the six phase-2 weeks.\n\nAgainst the break-even figure of 1.50 pp in the rollout memo, Boost clears the bar by a wide margin.\n\n**Recommendation: roll out.**\n\n## Method\n\nPhase 2 randomised Boost per order, so the boost and control arms are directly comparable. We take the\nlate-delivery rate in each arm over the metric population and difference them, with a two-proportion 95 %\ninterval. Reproduce with `python -m northline_eval readout`.\n\n## Checks\n\n| check | result |\n|---|---|\n| Sample ratio, realised vs configured share, 96 market-weeks | max abs z = 2.09 — no mismatch |\n| Pre-programme balance, enrolled vs never-enrolled markets | 14.55 % vs 15.30 % late — comparable |\n| Per-market consistency, all 16 markets | every market negative, range −3.4 to −4.6 pp |\n| Courier hours available per arm | boost 9.53 h, control 9.54 h, gap −0.08 % |\n\nThe courier-hours check is the one we were asked about most. Courier supply is the obvious thing that could\ndiffer between the arms, and it does not: the two arms draw on the same courier hours to within a tenth of a\npercent. **Capacity is therefore not confounding the comparison.**\n\n## Control arm against the never-enrolled markets\n\nThe memo requires this alongside the headline. The phase-2 control arm ran at 17.26 % late against 14.82 % in\nthe four never-enrolled markets, a difference of +2.44 pp.\n\nWe do not read this as evidence about Boost. The never-enrolled markets were never part of the programme;\nthey were not drawn against the enrolled markets and they are not a control group. A difference in level\nbetween two groups that were never randomised against each other is not an effect, and we would not report it\nas one.\n\n## Limitations\n\nSix weeks of phase 2. We have not modelled courier-side effects beyond the capacity check above.\n=== notebooks/analyst_note.md\n# Working note — Boost readout\n\n**Why the phase-2 design is the one to use.** Phase 1 was a three-week soak in eight markets, scheduled to\ncatch payout and fraud incidents before we exposed the whole estate. It is only three weeks, it is sixteen\nmarkets at best, and a market-level comparison across eight and eight gives an interval several points wide —\nuseless for a committee decision. Phase 2 randomised per order over six weeks and 120k orders, which is why\nthe interval is four tenths of a point rather than several points. I used phase 2.\n\n**Things I checked and then stopped worrying about.**\n\n- Assignment looks clean. Realised boost share tracks the configured target in every market-week; the largest\n  z across 96 market-weeks is 2.09, which is what you expect from 96 draws.\n- The enrolled and never-enrolled markets were comparable before the programme opened (14.55 vs 15.30 % late).\n- The effect is present in all sixteen markets, between −3.4 and −4.6 pp. A single market or a single week is\n  not driving it.\n- Courier supply per arm is identical to within 0.08 %. This was the one I expected to find something in, and\n  there is nothing there, so whatever Boost is doing it is not doing it by changing how many couriers we have.\n\n**Open.** The control arm is 2.4 pp worse than the never-enrolled markets. Those markets were never\nrandomised against the enrolled ones, so I do not think a level gap between them tells us anything, and I have\nleft it at that. Worth a second look if anyone has time before the committee, but it does not change the\nheadline.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

northline_eval/effects.py\n\"\"\"Programme effect and the checks the programme readout reports.\"\"\"\nimport math\n\nimport pandas as pd\n\nfrom northline_eval import panel, warehouse\n\n\ndef phase2_frame(con):\n    \"\"\"Metric-population phase-2 orders in enrolled markets, with their arm.\"\"\"\n    o = warehouse.orders(con)\n    run, hold, weeks = panel.phase2_markets(con)\n    f = o[o[\"market_id\"].isin(run) & o[\"week_start\"].isin(weeks)]\n    return f[f[\"arm\"].isin([\"boost\", \"control\"])].copy()\n\n\ndef arm_contrast(con):\n    \"\"\"Late-rate difference between the phase-2 arms, with a two-proportion interval.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    b = f[f[\"arm\"] == \"boost\"][\"is_late\"]\n    c = f[f[\"arm\"] == \"control\"][\"is_late\"]\n    diff = (b.mean() - c.mean()) * 100.0\n    se = math.sqrt(b.var(ddof=1) / len(b) + c.var(ddof=1) / len(c)) * 100.0\n    return dict(effect_pp=diff, ci_low_pp=diff - 1.96 * se, ci_high_pp=diff + 1.96 * se,\n                n_boost=int(len(b)), n_control=int(len(c)), n=int(len(f)))\n\n\ndef sample_ratio_check(con):\n    \"\"\"Realised boost share against the configured target, per market-week.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    cfg = warehouse.config(con)\n    cfg = cfg[cfg[\"phase\"] == \"phase2_order_randomised\"][[\"market_id\", \"week_start\",\n                                                          \"boost_share_target\"]]\n    got = (f.assign(b=(f[\"arm\"] == \"boost\").astype(int))\n             .groupby([\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], as_index=False)\n             .agg(n=(\"b\", \"size\"), realised=(\"b\", \"mean\")))\n    m = got.merge(cfg, on=[\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], how=\"left\")\n    m[\"z\"] = (m[\"realised\"] - m[\"boost_share_target\"]) / (\n        (m[\"boost_share_target\"] * (1 - m[\"boost_share_target\"]) / m[\"n\"]) ** 0.5)\n    return dict(max_abs_z=float(m[\"z\"].abs().max()), market_weeks=int(len(m)))\n\n\ndef pre_period_balance(con):\n    \"\"\"Pre-programme late rate of the enrolled markets against the never-enrolled markets.\"\"\"\n    b = warehouse.baseline(con)\n    run, hold, _ = panel.phase2_markets(con)\n    b[\"late_rate_pct\"] = b[\"late_orders\"] / b[\"orders\"] * 100.0\n    e = b[b[\"market_id\"].isin(run)]\n    h = b[b[\"market_id\"].isin(hold)]\n    return Phase 1 has no assignment rows: Boost was on\n    for every eligible order in the market-weeks configured with `boost_share_target = 1` (confirmed against\n    `incentive_ledger`, whose boosted_orders equal the eligible delivered orders in those market-weeks).\n    \"\"\"\n    c = warehouse.config(con)\n    p1_on = c[(c[\"phase\"] == \"phase1_soak\") & (c[\"boost_share_target\"] >= 1.0)][[\"market_id\", \"week_start\"]]\n    p1_on = set(map(tuple, p1_on.to_numpy()))\n    in_p1_on = [(m, w) in p1_on for m, w in zip(o[\"market_id\"], o[\"week_start\"])]\n    return (o[\"arm\"] == \"boost\") | pd.Series(in_p1_on, index=o.index)\n\n\ndef market_week(con):\n    o = warehouse.orders(con)\n    o[\"boosted\"] = boosted(con, o).astype(int)\n    ch = warehouse.courier_hours(con)\n    g = o.groupby([\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], as_index=False).agg(\n        orders=(\"order_id\", \"size\"), late_orders=(\"is_late\", \"sum\"), boost_share=(\"boosted\", \"mean\"))\n    g[\"late_rate_pct\"] = g[\"late_orders\"] / g[\"orders\"] * 100.0\n    hrs = ch.groupby([\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], as_index=False)[\"courier_hours\"].sum()\n    g = g.merge(hrs, \"week_start\"]).reset_index(drop=True)\n\n\ndef phase2_markets(con):\n    c = warehouse.config(con)\n    p2 = c[c[\"phase\"] == \"phase2_order_randomised\"]\n    return (sorted(p2[p2[\"status\"] == \"running\"][\"market_id\"].unique().tolist()),\n            sorted(p2[p2[\"status\"] == \"holdout\"][\"market_id\"].unique().tolist()),\n            sorted(p2[\"week_start\"].unique().tolist()))\n\n\ndef phase1_design(con):\n    \"\"\"Phase-1 market randomisation: (on markets, off markets, phase-1 weeks, pre-programme weeks).\"\"\"\n    c = <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Programme effect and the checks the readout reports.\n\nThe rollout memo asks for the change in the estate-wide late rate between Boost on every eligible order and\nBoost on none. The phase-2 order-level arm contrast does not estimate that: Boost offers are placed ahead of\nevery unboosted offer in the same market-hour queue (docs/dispatch_offer_queue.md), so boosted orders gain\npartly at the expense of control orders in the same market, and courier supply responds to market-level\nearnings (docs/courier_supply_note.md), which the order arms share. Both arms of phase 2 sit in a partly\nboosted market; neither is \"Boost on none\" or \"Boost on all\".\n\nPhase 1 randomised whole markets to all-on or all-off, which is the contrast the memo names. The estimate is\na market-level difference in differences: each enrolled market's phase-1 late rate minus its own\npre-programme late rate (markets differ persistently, docs/experiment_plan.md), averaged within arm with\nthe memo's pre-programme order-share weights, on minus off. Inference is over the 16 randomised markets.\n\"\"\"\nimport itertools\nimport math\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom scipy import stats\n\nfrom northline_eval import panel, warehouse\n\n\ndef phase2_frame(con):\n    \"\"\"Metric-population phase-2 orders in enrolled markets, with their arm.\"\"\"\n    o = warehouse.orders(con)\n    run, hold, weeks = panel.phase2_markets(con)\n    f = o[o[\"market_id\"].isin(run) & o[\"week_start\"].isin(weeks)]\n    return f[f[\"arm\"].isin([\"boost\", \"control\"])].copy()\n\n\ndef arm_contrast(con):\n    \"\"\"Late-rate difference between the phase-2 arms, with a two-proportion interval.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    b = f[f[\"arm\"] == \"boost\"][\"is_late\"]\n    c = f[f[\"arm\"] == \"control\"][\"is_late\"]\n    diff = (b.mean() - c.mean()) * 100.0\n    se = math.sqrt(b.var(ddof=1) / len(b) + c.var(ddof=1) / len(c)) * 100.0\n    return dict(effect_pp=diff, ci_low_pp=diff - 1.96 * se, ci_high_pp=diff + 1.96 * se,\n                n_boost=int(len(b)), n_control=int(len(c)), n=int(len(f)))\n\n\ndef phase1_market_frame(con, mw=None):\n    \"\"\"One row per enrolled market: pre and phase-1 totals, arm, and pre-programme estate weight.\"\"\"\n    mw = panel.market_week(con) if mw is None else mw\n    on, off, p1_weeks, pre_weeks = panel.phase1_design(con)\n    period = np.where(mw[\"week_start\"].isin(pre_weeks), \"pre\",\n                      np.where(mw[\"week_start\"].isin(p1_weeks), \"p1\", None))\n    m = mw.assign(period=period).dropna(subset=[\"period\"])\n    m = m[m[\"market_id\"].isin(on + off)]\n    g = m.groupby([\"market_id\", \"period\"])[[\"orders\", \"late_orders\", \"courier_hours\"]].sum().unstack()\n    f = pd.DataFrame({\n        \"on\": g.index.isin(on).astype(int),\n        \"late_pre_pct\": g[\"late_orders\"][\"pre\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"] * 100.0,\n        \"late_p1_pct\": g[\"late_orders\"][\"p1\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"p1\"] * 100.0,\n        \"hpo_pre\": g[\"courier_hours\"][\"pre\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"],\n        \"hpo_p1\": g[\"courier_hours\"][\"p1\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"p1\"],\n        \"weight\": g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"].sum(),\n    }, index=g.index)\n    f[\"late_change_pp\"] = f[\"late_p1_pct\"] - f[\"late_pre_pct\"]\n    f[\"log_hpo_change\"] = np.log(f[\"hpo_p1\"] / f[\"hpo_pre\"])\n    return f\n\n\ndef _weighted_contrast(y, z, w):\n    return np.average(y[z == 1], weights=w[z == 1]) - np.average(y[z == 0], weights=w[z == 0])\n\n\ndef _weighted_contrast_se(y, z, w):\n    \"\"\"HC2 standard error of the arm coefficient in a weighted regression of y on the arm indicator.\"\"\"\n    X = np.column_stack([np.ones_like(y), z])\n    W = np.diag(w)\n    bread = np.linalg.inv(X.T @ W @ X)\n    beta = bread @ X.T @ W @ y\n    resid = y - X @ beta\n    lev = np.einsum(\"ij,jk,ik->i\", X, bread, X) * w\n    meat = X.T @ np.diag((w * resid) ** 2 / (1.0 - lev)) @ X\n    return float(math.sqrt((bread @ meat @ bread)[1, 1]))\n\n\ndef programme_effect(con, mw=None):\n    \"\"\"Estate-wide all-on minus all-off late rate, pp, from the phase-1 market randomisation.\"\"\"\n    f = phase1_market_frame(con, mw)\n    y, z, w = f[\"late_change_pp\"].to_numpy(), f[\"on\"].to_numpy(), f[\"weight\"].to_numpy()\n    est = float(_weighted_contrast(y, z, w))\n    se = _weighted_contrast_se(y, z, w)\n    n = len(f)\n    q = stats.t.ppf(0.975, n - 2)\n    # Randomisation-inference p-value over every 8-of-16 assignment, as a design-based cross-check.\n    k = int(z.sum())\n    draws = []\n    for idx in itertools.combinations(range(n), k):\n        zz = np.zeros(n, dtype=int); zz[list(idx)] = 1\n        draws.append(_weighted_contrast(y, zz, w))\n    ri_p = float(np.mean(np.abs(np.array(draws)) >= abs(est) - 1e-12))\n    return dict(effect_pp=est, se_pp=se, ci_low_pp=est - q * se, ci_high_pp=est + q * se, n_units=n,\n                n_on=k, n_off=n - k, randomisation_p=ri_p,\n                post_only_pp=float(_weighted_contrast(f[\"late_p1_pct\"].to_numpy(), z, w)))\n\n\ndef courier_hours_response(con, mw=None):\n    \"\"\"% change in courier hours per order, all-on vs all-off, phase-1 market difference in differences.\"\"\"\n    f = phase1_market_frame(con, mw)\n    d = _weighted_contrast(f[\"log_hpo_change\"].to_numpy(), f[\"on\"].to_numpy(), f[\"weight\"].to_numpy())\n    return float((math.exp(d) - 1.0) * 100.0)\n\n\ndef sample_ratio_check(con):\n    \"\"\"Realised boost share against the configured target, per market-week.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    cfg = warehouse.config(con)\n    cfg = cfg[cfg[\"phase\"] == \"phase2_order_randomised\"][[\"market_id\", \"week_start\",\n                                                          \"boost_share_target\"]]\n    got = (f.assign(b=(f[\"arm\"] == \"boost\").astype(int))\n             .groupby([\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], as_index=False)\n             .agg(n=(\"b\", \"size\"), realised=(\"b\", \"mean\")))\n    m = got.merge(cfg, on=[\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], how=\"left\")\n    m[\"z\"] = (m[\"realised\"] - m[\"boost_share_target\"]) / (\n        (m[\"boost_share_target\"] * (1 - m[\"boost_share_target\"]) / m[\"n\"]) ** 0.5)\n    return dict(max_abs_z=float(m[\"z\"].abs().max()), market_weeks=int(len(m)))\n\n\ndef courier_hours_by_arm(con):\n    \"\"\"Courier hours in the market-hour of each phase-2 arm's orders. Both arms share the same pool, so\n    this is equal by construction and says nothing about whether Boost changes supply.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    ch = warehouse.courier_hours(con)\n    key = [\"market_id\", \"order_date\", \"hour\"]\n    h = ch.rename(columns={\"shift_date\": \"order_date\", \"hour_start\": \"hour\"})\n    j = f.merge(h[key + [\"courier_hours\"]], on=key, how=\"left\")\n    per = j.groupby(\"arm\")[\"courier_hours\"].mean()\n    boost, control = float(per.get(\"boost\", float(\"nan\"))), float(per.get(\"control\", float(\"nan\")))\n    return dict(boost=boost, control=control,\n                pct_gap=(boost / control - 1.0) * 100.0 if control else float(\"nan\"))\n\n\ndef control_vs_holdout(con):\n    \"\"\"Phase-2 control-arm late rate against the never-enrolled markets over the same weeks.\"\"\"\n    o = warehouse.orders(con)\n    run, hold, weeks = panel.phase2_markets(con)\n    ctl = o[(o[\"market_id\"].isin(run)) & (o[\"week_start\"].isin(weeks)) & (o[\"arm\"] == \"control\")]\n    ho = o[(o[\"market_id\"].isin(hold)) & (o[\"week_start\"].isin(weeks))]\n    return dict(control_late_pct=float(ctl[\"is_late\"].mean() * 100),\n                holdout_late_pct=float(ho[\"is_late\"].mean() * 100),\n                difference_pp=float((ctl[\"is_late\"].mean() - ho[\"is_late\"].mean()) * 100))\n\n\ndef by_market(con):\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    g = (f.groupby([\"market_id\", \"arm\"])[\"is_late\"].mean().unstack() * 100.0)\n    g[\"effect_pp\"] = g[\"boost\"] - g[\"control\"]\n    return g.reset_index()\nEOF\ncat > northline_eval/report.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Write the readout contract artefacts.\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport os\n\nfrom northline_eval import effects, panel\n\nBREAK_EVEN_PP = 0.19 / 12.70 * 100.0   # docs/rollout_decision_memo.md: 1.4961 pp\n\n\ndef decide(effect_pp, ci_low_pp, ci_high_pp):\n    \"\"\"Memo rule: a reduction of at least break-even, and a 95 % interval that excludes zero.\"\"\"\n    excludes_zero = ci_high_pp < 0 or ci_low_pp > 0\n    return \"roll_out\" if (effect_pp <= -BREAK_EVEN_PP and excludes_zero) else \"do_not_roll_out\"\n\n\ndef run(con, out_dir):\n    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)\n    mw = panel.market_week(con)\n    mw.to_csv(os.path.join(out_dir, \"market_week_panel.csv\"), index=False,\n              columns=[\"market_id\", \"week_start\", \"orders\", \"late_orders\", \"late_rate_pct\",\n                       \"boost_share\", \"courier_hours\"])\n    ac = effects.arm_contrast(con)\n    pe = effects.programme_effect(con, mw)\n    readout = {\n        \"orders_analysed\": ac[\"n\"],\n        \"order_arm_contrast_pp\": round(ac[\"effect_pp\"], 4),\n        \"programme_effect_pp\": round(pe[\"effect_pp\"], 4),\n        \"programme_effect_ci_low_pp\": round(pe[\"ci_low_pp\"], 4),\n        \"programme_effect_ci_high_pp\": round(pe[\"ci_high_pp\"], 4),\n        \"inference_unit_n\": pe[\"n_units\"],\n        \"courier_hours_response_pct\": round(effects.courier_hours_response(con, mw), 4),\n        \"decision\": decide(pe[\"effect_pp\"], pe[\"ci_low_pp\"], pe[\"ci_high_pp\"]),\n    }\n    with open(os.path.join(out_dir, \"readout.json\"), \"w\") as fh:\n        json.dump(readout, fh, indent=1, sort_keys=True)\n    return readout\nEOF\npython - <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Programme effect and the checks the readout reports.\n\nThe rollout memo asks for the change in the estate-wide late rate between Boost on every eligible order and\nBoost on none. The phase-2 order-level arm contrast does not estimate that: Boost offers are placed ahead of\nevery unboosted offer in the same market-hour queue (docs/dispatch_offer_queue.md), so boosted orders gain\npartly at the expense of control orders in the same market, and courier supply responds to market-level\nearnings (docs/courier_supply_note.md), which the order arms share. Both arms of phase 2 sit in a partly\nboosted market; neither is \"Boost on none\" or \"Boost on all\".\n\nPhase 1 randomised whole markets to all-on or all-off, which is the contrast the memo names. The estimate is\na market-level difference in differences: each enrolled market's phase-1 late rate minus its own\npre-programme late rate (markets differ persistently, docs/experiment_plan.md), averaged within arm with\nthe memo's pre-programme order-share weights, on minus off. Inference is over the 16 randomised markets.\n\"\"\"\nimport itertools\nimport math\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom scipy import stats\n\nfrom northline_eval import panel, warehouse\n\n\ndef phase2_frame(con):\n    \"\"\"Metric-population phase-2 orders in enrolled markets, with their arm.\"\"\"\n    o = warehouse.orders(con)\n    run, hold, weeks = panel.phase2_markets(con)\n    f = o[o[\"market_id\"].isin(run) & o[\"week_start\"].isin(weeks)]\n    return f[f[\"arm\"].isin([\"boost\", \"control\"])].copy()\n\n\ndef arm_contrast(con):\n    \"\"\"Late-rate difference between the phase-2 arms, with a two-proportion interval.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    b = f[f[\"arm\"] == \"boost\"][\"is_late\"]\n    c = f[f[\"arm\"] == \"control\"][\"is_late\"]\n    diff = (b.mean() - c.mean()) * 100.0\n    se = math.sqrt(b.var(ddof=1) / len(b) + c.var(ddof=1) / len(c)) * 100.0\n    return dict(effect_pp=diff, ci_low_pp=diff - 1.96 * se, ci_high_pp=diff + 1.96 * se,\n                n_boost=int(len(b)), n_control=int(len(c)), n=int(len(f)))\n\n\ndef phase1_market_frame(con, mw=None):\n    \"\"\"One row per enrolled market: pre and phase-1 totals, arm, and pre-programme estate weight.\"\"\"\n    mw = panel.market_week(con) if mw is None else mw\n    on, off, p1_weeks, pre_weeks = panel.phase1_design(con)\n    period = np.where(mw[\"week_start\"].isin(pre_weeks), \"pre\",\n                      np.where(mw[\"week_start\"].isin(p1_weeks), \"p1\", None))\n    m = mw.assign(period=period).dropna(subset=[\"period\"])\n    m = m[m[\"market_id\"].isin(on + off)]\n    g = m.groupby([\"market_id\", \"period\"])[[\"orders\", \"late_orders\", \"courier_hours\"]].sum().unstack()\n    f = pd.DataFrame({\n        \"on\": g.index.isin(on).astype(int),\n        \"late_pre_pct\": g[\"late_orders\"][\"pre\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"] * 100.0,\n        \"late_p1_pct\": g[\"late_orders\"][\"p1\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"p1\"] * 100.0,\n        \"hpo_pre\": g[\"courier_hours\"][\"pre\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"],\n        \"hpo_p1\": g[\"courier_hours\"][\"p1\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"p1\"],\n        \"weight\": g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"].sum(),\n    }, index=g.index)\n    f[\"late_change_pp\"] = f[\"late_p1_pct\"] - f[\"late_pre_pct\"]\n    f[\"log_hpo_change\"] = np.log(f[\"hpo_p1\"] / f[\"hpo_pre\"])\n    return f\n\n\ndef _weighted_contrast(y, z, w):\n    return np.average(y[z == 1], weights=w[z == 1]) - np.average(y[z == 0], weights=w[z == 0])\n\n\ndef _weighted_contrast_se(y, z, w):\n    \"\"\"HC2 standard error of the arm coefficient in a weighted regression of y on the arm indicator.\"\"\"\n    X = np.column_stack([np.ones_like(y), z])\n    W = np.diag(w)\n    bread = np.linalg.inv(X.T @ W @ X)\n    beta = bread @ X.T @ W @ y\n    resid = y - X @ beta\n    lev = np.einsum(\"ij,jk,ik->i\", X, bread, X) * w\n    meat = X.T @ np.diag((w * resid) ** 2 / (1.0 - lev)) @ X\n    return float(math.sqrt((bread @ meat @ bread)[1, 1]))\n\n\ndef programme_effect(con, mw=None):\n    \"\"\"Estate-wide all-on minus all-off late rate, pp, from the phase-1 market randomisation.\"\"\"\n    f = phase1_market_frame(con, mw)\n    y, z, w = f[\"late_change_pp\"].to_numpy(), f[\"on\"].to_numpy(), f[\"weight\"].to_numpy()\n    est = float(_weighted_contrast(y, z, w))\n    se = _weighted_contrast_se(y, z, w)\n    n = len(f)\n    q = stats.t.ppf(0.975, n - Both arms share the same pool, so\n    this is equal by construction and says nothing about whether Boost changes supply.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    ch = warehouse.courier_hours(con)\n    key = [\"market_id\", \"order_date\", \"hour\"]\n    h = ch.rename(columns={\"shift_date\": \"order_date\", \"hour_start\": \"hour\"})\n    j = f.merge(h[key + [\"courier_hours\"]], on=key, how=\"left\")\n    per = j.groupby(\"arm\")[\"courier_hours\"].mean()\n    boost, control = float(per.get(\"boost\", float(\"nan\"))), float(per.get(\"control\", float(\"nan\")))\n    return dict(boost=boost, control=control,\n                pct_gap=(boost / control - \"boost\") | pd.Series(in_p1_on, index=o.index)\n\n\ndef market_week(con):\n    o = warehouse.orders(con)\n    o[\"boosted\"] = boosted(con, o).astype(int)\n    ch = warehouse.courier_hours(con)\n    g = o.groupby([\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], as_index=False).agg(\n        orders=(\"order_id\", \"size\"), late_orders=(\"is_late\", \"sum\"), boost_share=(\"boosted\", \"mean\"))\n    g[\"late_rate_pct\"] = g[\"late_orders\"] / g[\"orders\"] * 100.0\n    hrs = ch.groupby([\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], as_index=False)[\"courier_hours\"].sum()\n    g = g.merge(hrs, \"week_start\"]).reset_index(drop=True)\n\n\ndef phase2_markets(con):\n    c = warehouse.config(con)\n    p2 = c[c[\"phase\"] == \"phase2_order_randomised\"]\n    return (sorted(p2[p2[\"status\"] == \"running\"][\"market_id\"].unique().tolist()),\n            sorted(p2[p2[\"status\"] == \"holdout\"][\"market_id\"].unique().tolist()),\n            sorted(p2[\"week_start\"].unique().tolist()))\n\n\ndef phase1_design(con):\n    \"\"\"Phase-1 market randomisation: (on markets, off markets, phase-1 weeks, pre-programme weeks).\"\"\"\n    c = warehouse.config(con)\n    p1 = c[(c[\"phase\"] == \"phase1_soak\") & (c[\"status\"] == \"running\")]\n    share = p1.groupby(\"market_id\")[\"boost_share_target\"].agg([\"min\", \"max\"])\n    assert (share[\"min\"] == share[\"max\"]).all(), \"phase-1 assignment varies within a market\"\n    pre = c[c[\"phase\"] == \"pre\"]\n    return (sorted(share.index[share[\"max\"] >= 1.0]), <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Programme effect and the checks the readout reports.\n\nThe rollout memo asks for the change in the estate-wide late rate between Boost on every eligible order and\nBoost on none. The phase-2 order-level arm contrast does not estimate that: Boost offers are placed ahead of\nevery unboosted offer in the same market-hour queue (docs/dispatch_offer_queue.md), so boosted orders gain\npartly at the expense of control orders in the same market, and courier supply responds to market-level\nearnings (docs/courier_supply_note.md), which the order arms share. Both arms of phase 2 sit in a partly\nboosted market; neither is \"Boost on none\" or \"Boost on all\".\n\nPhase 1 randomised whole markets to all-on or all-off, which is the contrast the memo names. The estimate is\na market-level difference in differences: each enrolled market's phase-1 late rate minus its own\npre-programme late rate (markets differ persistently, docs/experiment_plan.md), averaged within arm with\nthe memo's pre-programme order-share weights, on minus off. Inference is over the 16 randomised markets.\n\"\"\"\nimport itertools\nimport math\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom scipy import stats\n\nfrom northline_eval import panel, warehouse\n\n\ndef phase2_frame(con):\n    \"\"\"Metric-population phase-2 orders in enrolled markets, with their arm.\"\"\"\n    o = warehouse.orders(con)\n    run, hold, weeks = panel.phase2_markets(con)\n    f = o[o[\"market_id\"].isin(run) & o[\"week_start\"].isin(weeks)]\n    return f[f[\"arm\"].isin([\"boost\", \"control\"])].copy()\n\n\ndef arm_contrast(con):\n    \"\"\"Late-rate difference between the phase-2 arms, with a two-proportion interval.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    b = f[f[\"arm\"] == \"boost\"][\"is_late\"]\n    c = f[f[\"arm\"] == \"control\"][\"is_late\"]\n    diff = (b.mean() - c.mean()) * 100.0\n    se = math.sqrt(b.var(ddof=1) / len(b) + c.var(ddof=1) / len(c)) * 100.0\n    return dict(effect_pp=diff, ci_low_pp=diff - 1.96 * se, ci_high_pp=diff + 1.96 * se,\n                n_boost=int(len(b)), n_control=int(len(c)), n=int(len(f)))\n\n\ndef phase1_market_frame(con, mw=None):\n    \"\"\"One row per enrolled market: pre and phase-1 totals, arm, and pre-programme estate weight.\"\"\"\n    mw = panel.market_week(con) if mw is None else mw\n    on, off, p1_weeks, pre_weeks = panel.phase1_design(con)\n    period = np.where(mw[\"week_start\"].isin(pre_weeks), \"pre\",\n                      np.where(mw[\"week_start\"].isin(p1_weeks), \"p1\", None))\n    m = mw.assign(period=period).dropna(subset=[\"period\"])\n    m = m[m[\"market_id\"].isin(on + off)]\n    g = m.groupby([\"market_id\", \"period\"])[[\"orders\", \"late_orders\", \"courier_hours\"]].sum().unstack()\n    f = pd.DataFrame({\n        \"on\": g.index.isin(on).astype(int),\n        \"late_pre_pct\": g[\"late_orders\"][\"pre\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"] * 100.0,\n        \"late_p1_pct\": g[\"late_orders\"][\"p1\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"p1\"] * 100.0,\n        \"hpo_pre\": g[\"courier_hours\"][\"pre\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"],\n        \"hpo_p1\": g[\"courier_hours\"][\"p1\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"p1\"],\n        \"weight\": g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"] / g[\"orders\"][\"pre\"].sum(),\n    }, index=g.index)\n    f[\"late_change_pp\"] = f[\"late_p1_pct\"] - f[\"late_pre_pct\"]\n    f[\"log_hpo_change\"] = np.log(f[\"hpo_p1\"] / f[\"hpo_pre\"])\n    return f\n\n\ndef _weighted_contrast(y, z, w):\n    return np.average(y[z == 1], weights=w[z == 1]) - np.average(y[z == 0], weights=w[z == 0])\n\n\ndef _weighted_contrast_se(y, z, w):\n    \"\"\"HC2 standard error of the arm coefficient in a weighted regression of y on the arm indicator.\"\"\"\n    X = np.column_stack([np.ones_like(y), z])\n    W = np.diag(w)\n    bread = np.linalg.inv(X.T @ W @ X)\n    beta = bread @ X.T @ W @ y\n    resid = y - X @ beta\n    lev = np.einsum(\"ij,jk,ik->i\", X, bread, X) * w\n    meat = X.T @ np.diag((w * resid) ** 2 / (1.0 - lev)) @ X\n    return float(math.sqrt((bread @ meat @ bread)[1, 1]))\n\n\ndef programme_effect(con, mw=None):\n    \"\"\"Estate-wide all-on minus all-off late rate, pp, from the phase-1 market randomisation.\"\"\"\n    f = phase1_market_frame(con, mw)\n    y, z, w = f[\"late_change_pp\"].to_numpy(), f[\"on\"].to_numpy(), f[\"weight\"].to_numpy()\n    est = float(_weighted_contrast(y, z, w))\n    se = _weighted_contrast_se(y, z, w)\n    n = len(f)\n    q = stats.t.ppf(0.975, n - 2)\n    # Randomisation-inference p-value over every 8-of-16 assignment, as a design-based cross-check.\n    k = int(z.sum())\n    draws = []\n    for idx in itertools.combinations(range(n), k):\n        zz = np.zeros(n, dtype=int); zz[list(idx)] = 1\n        draws.append(_weighted_contrast(y, zz, w))\n    ri_p = float(np.mean(np.abs(np.array(draws)) >= abs(est) - 1e-12))\n    return dict(effect_pp=est, se_pp=se, ci_low_pp=est - q * se, ci_high_pp=est + q * se, n_units=n,\n                n_on=k, n_off=n - k, randomisation_p=ri_p,\n                post_only_pp=float(_weighted_contrast(f[\"late_p1_pct\"].to_numpy(), z, w)))\n\n\ndef courier_hours_response(con, mw=None):\n    \"\"\"% change in courier hours per order, all-on vs all-off, phase-1 market difference in differences.\"\"\"\n    f = phase1_market_frame(con, mw)\n    d = _weighted_contrast(f[\"log_hpo_change\"].to_numpy(), f[\"on\"].to_numpy(), f[\"weight\"].to_numpy())\n    return float((math.exp(d) - 1.0) * 100.0)\n\n\ndef sample_ratio_check(con):\n    \"\"\"Realised boost share against the configured target, per market-week.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    cfg = warehouse.config(con)\n    cfg = cfg[cfg[\"phase\"] == \"phase2_order_randomised\"][[\"market_id\", \"week_start\",\n                                                          \"boost_share_target\"]]\n    got = (f.assign(b=(f[\"arm\"] == \"boost\").astype(int))\n             .groupby([\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], as_index=False)\n             .agg(n=(\"b\", \"size\"), realised=(\"b\", \"mean\")))\n    m = got.merge(cfg, on=[\"market_id\", \"week_start\"], how=\"left\")\n    m[\"z\"] = (m[\"realised\"] - m[\"boost_share_target\"]) / (\n        (m[\"boost_share_target\"] * (1 - m[\"boost_share_target\"]) / m[\"n\"]) ** 0.5)\n    return dict(max_abs_z=float(m[\"z\"].abs().max()), market_weeks=int(len(m)))\n\n\ndef courier_hours_by_arm(con):\n    \"\"\"Courier hours in the market-hour of each phase-2 arm's orders. Both arms share the same pool, so\n    this is equal by construction and says nothing about whether Boost changes supply.\"\"\"\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    ch = warehouse.courier_hours(con)\n    key = [\"market_id\", \"order_date\", \"hour\"]\n    h = ch.rename(columns={\"shift_date\": \"order_date\", \"hour_start\": \"hour\"})\n    j = f.merge(h[key + [\"courier_hours\"]], on=key, how=\"left\")\n    per = j.groupby(\"arm\")[\"courier_hours\"].mean()\n    boost, control = float(per.get(\"boost\", float(\"nan\"))), float(per.get(\"control\", float(\"nan\")))\n    return dict(boost=boost, control=control,\n                pct_gap=(boost / control - 1.0) * 100.0 if control else float(\"nan\"))\n\n\ndef control_vs_holdout(con):\n    \"\"\"Phase-2 control-arm late rate against the never-enrolled markets over the same weeks.\"\"\"\n    o = warehouse.orders(con)\n    run, hold, weeks = panel.phase2_markets(con)\n    ctl = o[(o[\"market_id\"].isin(run)) & (o[\"week_start\"].isin(weeks)) & (o[\"arm\"] == \"control\")]\n    ho = o[(o[\"market_id\"].isin(hold)) & (o[\"week_start\"].isin(weeks))]\n    return dict(control_late_pct=float(ctl[\"is_late\"].mean() * 100),\n                holdout_late_pct=float(ho[\"is_late\"].mean() * 100),\n                difference_pp=float((ctl[\"is_late\"].mean() - ho[\"is_late\"].mean()) * 100))\n\n\ndef by_market(con):\n    f = phase2_frame(con)\n    g = (f.groupby([\"market_id\", \"arm\"])[\"is_late\"].mean().unstack() * 100.0)\n    g[\"effect_pp\"] = g[\"boost\"] - g[\"control\"]\n    return g.reset_index()\nEOF\ncat > northline_eval/report.py <<'EOF'\n\"\"\"Write the readout contract artefacts.\"\"\"\nimport json\nimport os\n\nfrom northline_eval import effects, panel\n\nBREAK_EVEN_PP = 0.19 / 12.70 * 100.0   # docs/rollout_decision_memo.md: 1.4961 pp\n\n\ndef decide(effect_pp, ci_low_pp, ci_high_pp):\n    \"\"\"Memo rule: a reduction of at least break-even, and a 95 % interval that excludes zero.\"\"\"\n    excludes_zero = ci_high_pp < 0 or ci_low_pp > 0\n    return \"roll_out\" if (effect_pp <= -BREAK_EVEN_PP and excludes_zero) else \"do_not_roll_out\"\n\n\ndef run(con, out_dir):\n    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)\n    mw = panel.market_week(con)\n    mw.to_csv(os.path.join(out_dir, \"market_week_panel.csv\"), index=False,\n              columns=[\"market_id\", \"week_start\", \"orders\", \"late_orders\", \"late_rate_pct\",\n                       \"boost_share\", \"courier_hours\"])\n    ac = effects.arm_contrast(con)\n    pe = effects.programme_effect(con, mw)\n    readout = {\n        \"orders_analysed\": ac[\"n\"],\n        \"order_arm_contrast_pp\": round(ac[\"effect_pp\"], 4),\n        \"programme_effect_pp\": round(pe[\"effect_pp\"], 4),\n        \"programme_effect_ci_low_pp\": round(pe[\"ci_low_pp\"], 4),\n        \"programme_effect_ci_high_pp\": round(pe[\"ci_high_pp\"], 4),\n        \"inference_unit_n\": pe[\"n_units\"],\n        \"courier_hours_response_pct\": round(effects.courier_hours_response(con, mw), 4),\n        \"decision\": decide(pe[\"effect_pp\"], pe[\"ci_low_pp\"], pe[\"ci_high_pp\"]),\n    }\n    with open(os.path.join(out_dir, \"readout.json\"), \"w\") as fh:\n        json.dump(readout, fh, indent=1, sort_keys=True)\n    return readout\nEOF\npython - {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfbBcPidisyL5bGit7nSD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Under the memo's rule the decision is **`do_not_roll_out`**. When you measure what the committee is actually deciding, Boost doesn't reduce late deliveries. The best estimate is that it makes them slightly worse, and the 95% interval runs from a 1.5 pp improvement to a 4.0 pp deterioration. I re-ran `python -m northline_eval readout --db data/northline.sqlite --out out` from scratch and it writes both output files.\n\n| | Value |\n|---|---|\n| `programme_effect_pp` (Boost on everywhere vs off everywhere) | **+1.24 pp** (95% interval −1.47 to +3.96) |\n| `inference_unit_n` | 16 markets |\n| `order_arm_contrast_pp` (the readout's method) | −5.12 pp |\n| `orders_analysed` | 115,424 |\n| `courier_hours_response_pct` | +3.28% courier hours per order |\n| Break-even | a fall of at least 1.4961 pp |\n\n**Why the readout's number is the wrong one.** The readout compares boosted and unboosted orders within the same markets in phase 2. That can't show what happens when Boost is on for every order, for two reasons in your own docs:\n- **Boost offers jump the queue.** `dispatch_offer_queue.md` says a Boost offer goes ahead of every unboosted offer in the same market and hour. Boosted orders get faster partly by making the unboosted ones slower. That gain disappears once every order is boosted. The comparison group shows the damage: unboosted orders in enrolled markets ran 3.5 pp later than the never-enrolled markets, whose pre-programme lateness was the same (18.87% vs 18.82%).\n- **Courier supply responds to the whole market.** `courier_supply_note.md` says couriers come online based on what a market is paying overall. Boosted and unboosted orders draw on the same couriers, so the readout's \"capacity is not confounding\" check is equal by construction and says nothing.\n\n**What I used instead.** Phase 1 assigned whole markets by coin flip: 8 had Boost on every eligible order and 8 had it on none, for four weeks. That is exactly the memo's \"all versus none\" comparison. For each market I took its phase-1 late rate minus its own pre-programme rate, since markets differ persistently. I then compared on-markets with off-markets, weighting each market by its pre-programme share of orders as the memo requires, and computed the interval over the 16 markets. A second test that reshuffles which markets were \"on\" in every possible way gives p = 0.32. In phase 1, on-markets got about 3.3% more courier hours per order and were still not on time more often.\n\n**Other points to know before you sign:**\n- **Phase 1 used for the estimate.** The analyst's note sets phase 1 aside as too small, saying its interval would be \"several points wide\". It is about 5.4 points wide. That reflects how little we know; it is not a reason to fall back on a precise answer to a different question.\n- **Simpler comparison.** If you skip the adjustment for each market's pre-programme rate, the estimate is −0.91 pp. That is still short of break-even, so the decision doesn't depend on that choice.\n- **The published readout doesn't reproduce.** Its own code gives −5.12 pp on 115,424 orders, not −4.0 pp on 120,671. Its checks table and its unboosted-vs-never-enrolled figures also differ from what the warehouse produces now.\n- **The pre-programme table counts on-time deliveries as late.** `market_week_baseline` treats deliveries arriving exactly at the promised time as late (9 orders in 8 market-weeks). The definition says \"exceeds\", so I computed everything from `orders`.\n- **Panel fix.** The old panel showed a boost share of 0 for the phase-1 \"on\" markets; they now show 1.0. The incentive ledger confirms every eligible order there was boosted.\n\nNothing under `data/` was changed, and no metric definitions, markets or weeks were altered or dropped. The changes are in `northline_eval/panel.py`, `effects.py` and `report.py`, plus one line in `cli.py`. The old phase-2 comparison is still reported as `order_arm_contrast_pp`, and the `checks` command now includes the new estimate."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":866,"cache_read_input_tokens":53365,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":866,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"678e3b94-195a-474e-8237-0b1ae1c201c6","uuid":"90a91b33-d852-46c5-9e8b-429e6aa9f041","timestamp":"2026-10-01T08:15:01.705Z","request_id":"req_011CfbBcNwkFFasqQb3PnccY"}
